Then, override only the functions you want to implement.\\n55: type Hook interface {\\n56: \\t// PreApply and PostApply are called before and after an action for a\\n57: \\t// single instance is applied. The sequence of these\\n71: \\t// for a given instance might look something like this:\\n72: \\t//\\n73: \\t//          PreProvisionInstance(aws_instance.foo[1], ...)\\n74: \\t//      PreProvisionInstanceStep(aws_instance.foo[1], \\\"file\\\")\\n75: \\t//     PostProvisionInstanceStep(aws_instance.foo[1], \\\"file\\\", nil)\\n76: \\t//      PreProvisionInstanceStep(aws_instance.foo[1], \\\"remote-exec\\\")\\n77: \\t//               ProvisionOutput(aws_instance.foo[1], \\\"remote-exec\\\", \\\"Installing foo...\\\")\\n78: \\t//               ProvisionOutput(aws_instance.foo[1], \\\"remote-exec\\\", \\\"Configuring bar...\\\")\\n79: \\t//     PostProvisionInstanceStep(aws_instance.foo[1], \\\"remote-exec\\\", nil)\\n80: \\t//         PostProvisionInstance(aws_instance.foo[1], ...)\\n81: \\t//\\n82: \\t// ProvisionOutput is called with output sent back by the provisioners.\\n83: \\t// This will be called multiple times as output comes in, with each call\\n84: \\t// representing one line of output. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-haiku-4-5-20251001","id":"msg_01P6fUvGZwNaohk4M7UGW27C","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01A3sL86C8gGxZcEqismwgkx","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/workspace/documentation.md","content":"# Terraform Plan/Apply Pipeline Architecture\n\n## Overview\n\nTerraform's plan and apply operations are orchestrated through a sophisticated dependency graph system that coordinates resource lifecycle management, provider interactions, and state synchronization. This document provides a detailed technical explanation of how these components interact to execute infrastructure changes.\n\n## 1. Graph Builder System\n\n### 1.1 Graph Architecture\n\nThe Terraform execution model is built around a **Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG)** that represents resources, data sources, modules, and their dependencies. This graph is the foundation of Terraform's execution model and enables:\n\n- **Concurrent execution** of independent nodes\n- **Dependency ordering** to ensure correct sequencing\n- **Partial execution** through targeted operations\n- **Resource expansion** for count/for_each resources\n\n### 1.2 Graph Builders\n\nTerraform provides specialized graph builders for different operation types:\n\n#### PlanGraphBuilder\n- **Purpose**: Constructs the execution graph for planning operations\n- **Input**: Current configuration, prior state, root module variables\n- **Key Fields**:\n  - `Config`: The desired infrastructure configuration\n  - `State`: Current infrastructure state\n  - `RootVariableValues`: User-provided variable inputs\n  - `Targets`: Optional resource filtering for targeted operations\n  - `ForceReplace`: Resource instances that must be replaced\n  - `ExternalProviderConfigs`: Pre-initialized provider instances\n\n- **Operation**: Makes decisions primarily based on configuration (desired state) rather than the current state\n- **Output**: An execution graph ready for the plan walk\n\n#### ApplyGraphBuilder\n- **Purpose**: Constructs the execution graph for applying a previously-generated plan\n- **Input**: Configuration, change set (diff), current state, plan variables\n- **Key Difference**: Operates from the plan diff rather than configuration, ensuring only changes explicitly in the plan are applied\n- **Protection Mechanism**: Prevents unintended modifications to resources not in the diff\n\n#### BasicGraphBuilder\n- **Purpose**: Generic graph construction framework\n- **Mechanism**: Executes a series of graph transformations (steps) sequentially\n- **Validation**: Automatically validates the resulting DAG is acyclic and valid\n\n### 1.3 Graph Transformation Pipeline\n\nGraph transformers modify and augment the graph through multiple sequential transformation steps. The pipeline follows a **visitor pattern** where each transformer can add nodes, remove nodes, and create edges.\n\n#### Core Transformation Types:\n\n**Configuration Transformers:**\n- `TransformConfig`: Attaches configuration blocks to resource nodes\n- `TransformProvider`: Adds provider configuration nodes\n- `TransformModule`: Handles module expansion and nesting\n\n**Reference Resolution:**\n- `TransformReference`: Analyzes code expressions to discover dependencies\n  - Identifies references from one resource to another\n  - Analyzes references in provisioner blocks, lifecycle rules, and count/for_each expressions\n  - Creates edges based on discovered dependencies\n\n**State and Schema Attachment:**\n- `TransformAttachState`: Attaches current state snapshots to nodes\n- `TransformAttachSchema`: Binds provider schemas to resource nodes\n- `TransformAttachConfigProvider`: Associates provider configurations with resources\n\n**Dynamic Expansion:**\n- `TransformExpand`: Handles resource instance expansion\n- `TransformResourceCount`: Expands resources with `count` meta-argument\n- `TransformModuleExpansion`: Expands module instances\n\n**Lifecycle Management:**\n- `TransformDestroyEdge`: Adds destruction dependency edges\n- `TransformDestroyPlannedDestroyCBD`: Handles create-before-destroy lifecycle rules\n- `TransformTransitiveReduction`: Optimizes graph by removing redundant transitive edges\n\n**Deferred Actions:**\n- `TransformDeferred`: Marks actions that cannot be evaluated as deferred\n\n**Orphan Handling:**\n- `TransformOrphanResource`: Identifies and marks removed resources for deletion\n- `TransformOrphanOutput`: Handles outputs removed from configuration\n\n### 1.4 Graph Node Types and Interfaces\n\nNodes in the graph represent different infrastructure objects and their operations:\n\n#### Base Node Types:\n- **Resources**: `nodeExpandPlannableResource`, `nodeResourcePlanInstance`, `nodeResourceApplyInstance`\n- **Modules**: `nodeModuleExpand`, represents module instantiation\n- **Providers**: `nodeProviderConfig`, manages provider initialization\n- **Variables**: `nodeRootVariable`, `nodeModuleVariable`, `nodeVariableValidation`\n- **Outputs**: `nodeOutput`, computes and manages outputs\n- **Locals**: `nodeLocal`, evaluates local values\n\n#### Node Interface Contracts:\n\n**GraphNodeReferenceable**\n```go\ntype GraphNodeReferenceable interface {\n    ReferenceableAddrs() []addrs.Referenceable\n}\n```\nIndicates that a node can be referenced by other nodes in expressions.\n\n**GraphNodeReferencer**\n```go\ntype GraphNodeReferencer interface {\n    References() []*addrs.Reference\n}\n```\nProvides the list of resources/variables this node depends on by analyzing its expressions.\n\n**GraphNodeDynamicExpandable**\nIndicates the node expands into multiple sub-nodes (e.g., count expansion):\n- Expansion happens during graph traversal\n- Sub-nodes are created dynamically based on variable values\n- Critical for handling `count` and `for_each` meta-arguments\n\n**GraphNodeExecutable**\n```go\ntype GraphNodeExecutable interface {\n    Execute(EvalContext, walkOperation) tfdiags.Diagnostics\n}\n```\nNodes implementing this interface contain the actual logic executed during graph walks.\n\n### 1.5 Dependency Discovery\n\nDependencies are discovered through multiple mechanisms:\n\n1. **Expression Analysis**: Terraform parses all HCL expressions to identify references\n   - Resource attribute references: `aws_instance.example.id`\n   - Data source references: `data.aws_ami.example.id`\n   - Variable references: `var.instance_type`\n   - Local value references: `local.common_tags`\n\n2. **Explicit Dependencies**: The `depends_on` meta-argument creates explicit dependency edges\n   - Used when implicit dependencies cannot be discovered\n   - Analyzed by `TransformReference` and `graphNodeDependsOn` interface\n\n3. **Provider Dependencies**: Implicit dependencies on provider configurations\n   - Every resource depends on its configured provider node\n   - Ensures providers are initialized before resource operations\n\n4. **Lifecycle Dependencies**: Special edges for create-before-destroy operations\n   - New resource must be created before old is destroyed\n   - Handled by `TransformDestroyEdge` and CBD transformers\n\n## 2. Provider Interface and Lifecycle\n\n### 2.1 Provider Plugin Architecture\n\nTerraform uses a **plugin-based architecture** for providers, communicating via **gRPC** with provider processes:\n\n#### Initialization Flow:\n1. **Plugin Loading**: Providers are loaded from disk or network based on lock file\n2. **Process Launch**: Each unique provider gets its own process\n3. **Handshake**: Terraform and provider negotiate protocol version\n4. **Schema Retrieval**: Terraform fetches resource types and data sources offered\n\n### 2.2 Provider Instance Management\n\n#### contextPlugins Structure:\nMaintains lifecycle of all provider instances across an operation:\n```\ncontextPlugins\n├── factories: map[addrs.Provider]providers.Factory\n├── instances: map[addrs.AbsProviderConfig]providers.Interface\n└── schemas: map[addrs.AbsProviderConfig]providers.ProviderSchema\n```\n\n#### Provider Lifecycle Phases:\n\n**1. Initialization (Plan/Apply Start)**\n- `EvalContext.InitProvider()` creates provider instance\n- Called once per provider configuration\n- Establishes gRPC connection to provider process\n- Panics if provider already initialized (prevents accidental duplicate initialization)\n\n**2. Configuration (Before Operations)**\n- `EvalContext.ConfigureProvider()` passes HCL configuration to provider\n- Configuration merged from:\n  - Explicit `provider` blocks\n  - User input via `provider_input()` function\n  - Environment variables\n- Returns diagnostics if configuration invalid\n\n**3. Operation**\n- Provider instance available via `EvalContext.Provider(addr)`\n- Returns `providers.Interface` implementing operations:\n  - `GetSchema()`: Retrieve resource/data source schemas\n  - `ValidateResourceConfig()`: Validate resource configuration\n  - `PlanResourceChange()`: Determine what will change\n  - `ApplyResourceChange()`: Execute the change\n  - `ReadDataSource()`: Fetch data source values\n  - Several other operations for refresh, import, etc.\n\n**4. Cleanup**\n- `EvalContext.CloseProvider()` closes provider connection\n- Called when provider no longer needed\n- Releases gRPC connection and cleans up resources\n\n### 2.3 Resource Operation Delegation\n\nResource operations are delegated through a standard flow:\n\n#### Plan Time (Provider Methods):\n1. **ReadResource**: Fetch current remote state for comparison\n2. **PlanResourceChange**: Provider determines what action is needed\n   - Inputs: prior state, proposed new config, config marks\n   - Outputs: planned new state, action (create/update/delete/no-op)\n   - Provider implements custom logic to detect drift\n\n#### Apply Time (Provider Methods):\n1. **ApplyResourceChange**: Execute the planned action\n   - Inputs: prior state, planned state\n   - Outputs: final state after operation\n   - Provider handles all cloud API interactions\n2. **ReadResource**: Verify final state was achieved\n\n### 2.4 Provider Configuration Resolution\n\nProvider configuration involves multiple layers of merging:\n\n**Configuration Sources (in precedence order):**\n1. **Explicit `provider` blocks**: Hardcoded in configuration\n2. **User input**: From `provider_input()` interactive prompts\n3. **Environment variables**: Provider-specific env var support\n4. **Defaults**: Provider applies default values\n\n**Multi-alias Providers:**\n- Single provider type can be configured multiple times with different aliases\n- Each configuration creates separate provider instance\n- Resources select provider via `provider = \"type.alias\"` argument\n\n### 2.5 Provider Schema Management\n\nProvider schemas are cached to avoid repeated round-trips:\n\n- **PreloadedProviderSchemas**: Optional optimization in `ContextOpts`\n  - Caller can pre-load schemas already fetched elsewhere\n  - Avoids redundant provider initialization\n  - Must be accurate or runtime behavior becomes erratic\n\n- **Schema Caching**: Schemas cached per provider configuration address\n  - Retrieved via `EvalContext.ProviderSchema(addr)`\n  - Used for configuration validation and type checking\n  - Critical for error messages and expression evaluation\n\n## 3. State Management\n\n### 3.1 State Structure\n\nTerraform maintains a hierarchical state representation:\n\n```\nState\n├── Version: int (schema version)\n├── Serial: int (monotonically incrementing change counter)\n├── Lineage: string (tracks state file identity across snapshots)\n├── Modules: map[addrs.ModuleInstance]Module\n│   └── Module (per module instance)\n│       ├── Outputs: map[string]OutputValue\n│       ├── Resources: map[string]Resource\n│       │   └── Resource\n│       │       └── Instances: map[addrs.InstanceKey]InstanceObject\n│       │           └── InstanceObject (per resource instance)\n│       │               ├── Status: (Ready, Tainted, Deposed)\n│       │               ├── AttrsJSON: []byte (actual resource attributes)\n│       │               ├── Private: []byte (provider-specific metadata)\n│       │               └── Deposed: []DeposedInstanceObject (for CBD)\n│       └── (nested modules)\n└── CheckResults: map[addrs.AbsResourceInstance]CheckState\n```\n\n**Key Concepts:**\n- **Status**: Indicates health of instance (Ready, Tainted, Deposed)\n- **AttrsJSON**: Resource attributes stored as JSON for schema-agnostic access\n- **Private**: Provider-specific data opaque to Terraform core\n- **Deposed**: Previous version kept during create-before-destroy operations\n- **CheckResults**: Results from resource checks (assertions)\n\n### 3.2 State Snapshots and Concurrency\n\nState access during graph walks is carefully managed:\n\n**Snapshot Mechanism:**\n- State is snapshotted at the beginning of walks\n- Nodes read from the snapshot, not live state\n- Prevents inconsistencies from concurrent updates\n- Final state computed at walk end and persisted\n\n**Synchronization:**\n- `states.SyncState` wrapper adds locking\n- Prevents concurrent modification of in-memory state\n- Serializes writes while allowing multiple readers\n\n**Concurrency Model:**\n- Multiple graph nodes execute in parallel\n- Each node reads snapshot, computes changes, queues writes\n- After all nodes complete, writes applied atomically\n- Results in consistent state even with parallel execution\n\n### 3.3 State Changes During Plan and Apply\n\n#### Plan Phase:\n1. **Refresh**: Fetch current remote values (if not skipped)\n   - Provider.ReadResource() called for each managed resource\n   - Current remote state becomes prior state for comparison\n2. **Plan**: Compare prior state with configuration\n   - Provider.PlanResourceChange() determines action needed\n   - Actions: Create, Read, Update, Delete, No-op, Replace\n3. **Plan File**: Actions serialized to plan file for apply phase\n   - Includes configuration, prior state, proposed changes\n   - Enables apply phase to reproduce plan consistently\n\n#### Apply Phase:\n1. **Validation**: Verify plan still applies to current state\n   - Checks for external changes since plan creation\n   - Detects conflicts that would invalidate the plan\n2. **Application**: Execute planned changes\n   - Provider.ApplyResourceChange() performs operations\n   - State updated with new values from provider\n3. **Persistence**: Final state written to state file\n   - Serial incremented\n   - Lineage preserved for tracking\n   - Stored in backend (local, remote, etc.)\n\n### 3.4 State Backends and Remote Storage\n\nState backends provide persistence mechanisms:\n\n**Backend Responsibilities:**\n- **State Lock**: Prevent concurrent modifications\n  - `StateLocker` interface provides distributed locks\n  - Critical for teams sharing infrastructure\n  - Ensures serial execution of operations\n- **Remote Persistence**: Store state durably\n  - S3, Terraform Cloud, Azure Storage, etc.\n  - Backup and version control built-in\n- **State Versioning**: Track state history\n  - Backend may keep historical versions\n  - Enables recovery from accidental changes\n\n**State Manager (`statemgr.State`):**\n- Wraps backend storage\n- Implements atomic read/write semantics\n- Handles lock acquisition before writes\n\n## 4. Execution Flow and Hook System\n\n### 4.1 Overall Execution Pipeline\n\n#### Command Invocation → Plan/Apply Cycle:\n\n```\nUser Command (terraform plan/apply)\n    ↓\nLoad Configuration\n    ↓\nLoad Prior State\n    ↓\nCreate Context\n    ├─ Initialize Plugins\n    ├─ Configure Providers\n    └─ Set up Evaluation Context\n    ↓\nBuild Graph (Plan-specific or Apply-specific)\n    ├─ Apply Transformations\n    ├─ Discover Dependencies\n    ├─ Expand Resources\n    └─ Validate DAG\n    ↓\nWalk Graph (Concurrent Execution)\n    ├─ Topological Ordering\n    ├─ Node Evaluation\n    ├─ Provider Operations\n    └─ State Updates\n    ↓\nReturn Results\n    ├─ Plan: Serialized change set\n    └─ Apply: Updated state\n```\n\n### 4.2 Graph Walking and Execution\n\n#### Walker Pattern\n\nThe **GraphWalker** interface orchestrates execution:\n\n```go\ntype GraphWalker interface {\n    EvalContext() EvalContext\n    enterScope(evalContextScope) EvalContext\n    exitScope(evalContextScope)\n    Execute(EvalContext, GraphNodeExecutable) tfdiags.Diagnostics\n}\n```\n\n**Responsibilities:**\n- Provides evaluation context to nodes\n- Manages scope entry/exit for module traversal\n- Executes individual nodes via `Execute()`\n- Aggregates diagnostics\n\n#### ContextGraphWalker Implementation:\n\nThe main implementation coordinating actual work:\n\n**EvalContext Provisioning:**\n- Creates evaluation context per node\n- Context provides access to:\n  - Current module path\n  - State, configuration, variables\n  - Provider instances\n  - Hook callbacks\n  - Expression evaluation\n\n**Node Execution:**\n1. Determines if node is executable (`GraphNodeExecutable`)\n2. Calls `Execute()` method with eval context\n3. Collects and aggregates diagnostics\n4. Propagates panics with context\n\n**Parallelism Control:**\n- `parallelSem`: Semaphore limiting concurrent executions\n- Default parallelism: 10 (configurable)\n- Limits CPU pressure and provider rate throttling\n- Each node acquisition blocks until semaphore permits\n\n#### DAG Walk Implementation\n\nThe `dag.Walk()` function implements parallel graph traversal:\n\n**Algorithm:**\n1. **Topological Sort**: Vertices with no dependencies ready first\n2. **Async Dispatch**: Ready vertices sent to worker goroutines\n3. **Dependency Tracking**: Maintains in-flight work count\n4. **Completion Notifications**: Workers signal completion\n5. **Deadlock Detection**: Identifies cycles and halts\n6. **Aggregation**: Collects all diagnostics\n\n**Key Properties:**\n- Fully asynchronous, maximizes parallelism\n- Respects dependency edges\n- Stops on first error (with graceful shutdown of in-flight nodes)\n- Provides panic safety through recovery handlers\n\n### 4.3 Hook System and Extension Points\n\nHooks provide extension points for observing and controlling execution:\n\n#### Hook Interface\n\n```go\ntype Hook interface {\n    // Plan/Apply lifecycle\n    PreApply(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, action, priorState, plannedNewState) (HookAction, error)\n    PostApply(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, newState, err error) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // Plan comparison (diff)\n    PreDiff(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, priorState, proposedNewState) (HookAction, error)\n    PostDiff(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, action, priorState, plannedNewState) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // Refresh operations\n    PreRefresh(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, priorState) (HookAction, error)\n    PostRefresh(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, priorState, newState) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // Provisioning\n    PreProvisionInstance(id HookResourceIdentity, state) (HookAction, error)\n    PostProvisionInstance(id HookResourceIdentity, state) (HookAction, error)\n    PreProvisionInstanceStep(id HookResourceIdentity, typeName) (HookAction, error)\n    PostProvisionInstanceStep(id HookResourceIdentity, typeName, err) (HookAction, error)\n    ProvisionOutput(id HookResourceIdentity, typeName, line string)\n\n    // Import operations\n    PreImportState(id HookResourceIdentity, importID) (HookAction, error)\n    PostImportState(id HookResourceIdentity, imported []ImportedResource) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // Action invocations (Terraform 1.10+)\n    StartAction(id HookActionIdentity) (HookAction, error)\n    ProgressAction(id HookActionIdentity, progress string) (HookAction, error)\n    CompleteAction(id HookActionIdentity, err error) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // State updates\n    PostStateUpdate(new *states.State) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // Shutdown signal\n    Stopping()\n}\n```\n\n#### Hook Action Control\n\n```go\ntype HookAction byte\n\nconst (\n    HookActionContinue HookAction = iota  // Continue normal execution\n    HookActionHalt                         // Halt immediately, cancel operation\n)\n```\n\nHooks can:\n- **Observe**: Log operations, collect metrics\n- **Control**: Return errors to halt execution\n- **Modify**: Update context during execution (in some hooks)\n- **Respond**: React to state updates\n\n#### Hook Registration and Invocation\n\n1. **Registration**: Hooks passed in `ContextOpts` during Context creation\n2. **Storage**: Stored in Context along with stop hook\n3. **Invocation**: Called via `EvalContext.Hook()` during node execution\n4. **Ordering**: All hooks called for each event (in registration order)\n5. **Aggregation**: First error halts execution, other hooks not called\n\n#### Internal Hooks\n\n**stopHook**: Built-in hook detecting graceful shutdown signals\n- Monitors SIGINT, SIGTERM signals\n- Sets stop context causing nodes to abort\n\n#### Use Cases\n\n- **UI Callbacks**: Update progress bars and status displays\n- **Logging**: Detailed operation logging\n- **Metrics**: Collect performance data\n- **Policy**: Enforce constraints (e.g., prevent certain resources)\n- **Testing**: Mock provider responses\n\n### 4.4 Plan-Specific Execution\n\n**PlanGraphBuilder** creates graph specific to planning:\n\n1. **Refresh**: Fetch current state (unless skipped)\n2. **Config Comparison**: Compare current with desired\n3. **Plan Generation**: Provider determines actions\n4. **Deferral Support**: Mark unplannable resources as deferred\n\n**Node Types Used:**\n- `nodeExpandPlannableResource`: Expansion for resources\n- `nodeResourcePlanInstance`: Planning for each instance\n- `nodeProviderConfig`: Provider initialization\n- `nodeRootVariable`: Variable evaluation\n- `nodeOutput`: Output value computation\n\n**Outputs:**\n- `plans.Plan` containing:\n  - Computed changes per resource\n  - Prior state snapshot\n  - Configuration snapshot\n  - Root variable values\n  - Timestamp for audit trail\n\n### 4.5 Apply-Specific Execution\n\n**ApplyGraphBuilder** creates graph specific to applying:\n\n1. **Validation**: Verify plan is still applicable\n2. **Provisioning**: Run provisioners after resources created\n3. **Application**: Execute provider operations\n4. **State Persistence**: Write updated state\n\n**Node Types Used:**\n- `nodeExpandApplyableResource`: Resource expansion\n- `nodeResourceApplyInstance`: Applying each instance\n- `nodeProviderConfig`: Provider initialization\n- `nodeProvisioner`: Provisioning execution\n\n**Key Differences from Plan:**\n- Operates on plan diff, not full configuration\n- Provider apply operations modify infrastructure\n- State written durably after completion\n- Provisions executed post-creation (if configured)\n\n### 4.6 Dynamic Expansion (count/for_each)\n\nResource instances are expanded dynamically during graph traversal:\n\n**Expansion Nodes:**\n- `nodeExpandPlannableResource` (plan phase)\n- `nodeExpandApplyableResource` (apply phase)\n\n**Mechanism:**\n1. **Parent Node**: Represents resource as a whole\n2. **Expansion Decision**: Evaluates `count` or `for_each` expression\n3. **Sub-node Creation**: Generates instance-specific nodes\n4. **Dynamic Dependency**: Instance sub-nodes added to graph dynamically\n5. **Parallel Execution**: Instances can execute in parallel\n\n**Dependency Propagation:**\n- Dependencies on unexpanded resource apply to all instances\n- Dependencies on specific instances specified via splat expressions\n- Inter-instance dependencies via implicit references\n\n### 4.7 Error Handling and Propagation\n\n**Diagnostic Aggregation:**\n- Each node execution returns `tfdiags.Diagnostics`\n- Collected in graph walk, returned to caller\n- Includes errors, warnings, and informational messages\n\n**Error Severity Levels:**\n- **Error**: Fatal, prevents apply execution\n- **Warning**: Non-fatal, operation continues\n- **Info**: Informational, for user awareness\n\n**Panic Safety:**\n- Panic in node execution caught and logged\n- Graph walk continues with other nodes\n- Panic converted to diagnostic error\n- Prevents entire process from crashing\n\n**Graceful Shutdown:**\n- On error, graph walk signals remaining nodes\n- Nodes check stop context, abort cleanly\n- In-flight operations completed or cancelled\n- Resources in partial states logged for recovery\n\n## 5. Component Integration\n\n### 5.1 Context as Central Coordinator\n\nThe `Context` struct serves as the central coordinator:\n\n```go\ntype Context struct {\n    meta *ContextMeta              // Working directory metadata\n    plugins *contextPlugins        // Provider/provisioner factories\n    hooks []Hook                   // Extension points\n    parallelSem Semaphore         // Concurrency control\n    runContext context.Context     // Cancellation support\n    // ...\n}\n```\n\n**Responsibilities:**\n- Owns plugins and hooks\n- Controls parallelism\n- Manages operation lifecycle\n- Coordinates graph building and walking\n\n### 5.2 EvalContext as State and Service Provider\n\nThe `EvalContext` provides all services nodes need:\n\n- **State Access**: Current and proposed state values\n- **Configuration**: Module configurations and variable values\n- **Provider Access**: Initialize, configure, and use providers\n- **Hook Dispatch**: Call registered hooks\n- **Expression Evaluation**: Evaluate HCL expressions\n- **Module Path**: Current position in module hierarchy\n\nImplemented by `ContextGraphWalker` during execution.\n\n### 5.3 Graph as Execution Plan\n\nThe `Graph` struct represents the execution plan:\n\n- **Vertices**: Individual infrastructure objects and operations\n- **Edges**: Dependency relationships\n- **Validation**: Ensures DAG structure (no cycles)\n- **Walking**: Traversal with node execution\n\n### 5.4 Plan as Bridge Between Phases\n\nThe `plans.Plan` struct bridges planning and application:\n\n- **Captures**: What will change and why\n- **Immutable**: Prevents accidental change between phases\n- **Serializable**: Can be reviewed, stored, transmitted\n- **Reproducible**: Apply reproduces plan consistently\n\n## 6. Advanced Topics\n\n### 6.1 Targeted Operations\n\n**Mechanism:**\n- User specifies resources to target via `-target` flag\n- `Targets` list passed to graph builders\n- `TransformTargets` removes untargeted resources from graph\n- Warnings issued that plan is incomplete\n\n**Use Cases:**\n- Rescue partial failures\n- Manage dependencies manually\n- Operations on single resources\n- **Warning**: Can cause inconsistencies if dependencies crossed\n\n### 6.2 Create-Before-Destroy (CBD)\n\n**Lifecycle Rule** for zero-downtime updates:\n\n1. **Detection**: Resource has `lifecycle { create_before_destroy = true }`\n2. **Graph Modification**: `TransformDestroyEdge` reorders dependencies\n3. **Execution**:\n   - New resource created first\n   - Old resource destroyed after new is ready\n   - Prevents service interruption\n\n### 6.3 Deferred Actions\n\n**Purpose**: Handle situations where operations cannot be planned yet\n\n**Triggers:**\n- Unknown values in `count` or `for_each` expressions\n- Provider-issued deferral notices\n- Data source dependencies on managed resources being created\n\n**Handling:**\n- Marked as deferred in plan\n- Not executed during this apply\n- Requires subsequent plan/apply to complete\n- Stored in state for next run\n\n### 6.4 Ephemeral Resources\n\n**Purpose**: Temporary resources that don't persist in state\n\n**Characteristics:**\n- Data source-like but for specific use cases\n- Values only exist during operation\n- Not stored in state\n- Recreated each plan/apply\n\n**Lifecycle:**\n- Opened at start of operation\n- Used by dependent resources\n- Closed at operation completion\n\n### 6.5 Resource Importing\n\n**Purpose**: Bring existing infrastructure under Terraform management\n\n**Flow:**\n- User specifies resource address and import ID\n- `nodeResourceImport` fetches remote state\n- Provider's `ReadResource` populates state with remote values\n- Configuration must match or manual editing required\n- State written for use in subsequent plans\n\n## Conclusion\n\nTerraform's plan/apply pipeline is a sophisticated system balancing:\n\n- **Correctness**: DAGs ensure proper ordering\n- **Performance**: Parallel execution maximizes throughput\n- **Reliability**: State snapshots and atomic updates ensure consistency\n- **Extensibility**: Hooks and interfaces enable customization\n- **Safety**: Immutable plans enable review before execution\n\nUnderstanding these architectural components is essential for:\n- Extending Terraform with custom providers\n- Building tools that invoke Terraform programmatically\n- Debugging complex infrastructure changes\n- Contributing to Terraform core development\n- Optimizing Terraform for specific use cases\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":12,"cache_creation_input_tokens":4687,"cache_read_input_tokens":67767,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":4687},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"22f83701-a397-4cb1-b7df-1ea733957c59","uuid":"d2b831b2-9c52-40bf-8306-470bd3acf199"}

This document provides a detailed technical explanation of how these components interact to execute infrastructure changes.\n\n## 1. Graph Builder System\n\n### 1.1 Graph Architecture\n\nThe Terraform execution model is built around a **Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG)** that represents resources, data sources, modules, and their dependencies. This graph is the foundation of Terraform's execution model and enables:\n\n- **Concurrent execution** of independent nodes\n- **Dependency ordering** to ensure correct sequencing\n- **Partial execution** through targeted operations\n- **Resource expansion** for count/for_each resources\n\n### 1.2 Graph Builders\n\nTerraform provides specialized graph builders for different operation types:\n\n#### PlanGraphBuilder\n- **Purpose**: Constructs the execution graph for planning operations\n- **Input**: Current configuration, prior state, root module variables\n- **Key Fields**:\n  - `Config`: The desired infrastructure configuration\n  - `State`: Current infrastructure state\n  - `RootVariableValues`: User-provided variable inputs\n  - `Targets`: Optional resource filtering for targeted operations\n  - `ForceReplace`: Resource instances that must be replaced\n  - `ExternalProviderConfigs`: Pre-initialized provider instances\n\n- **Operation**: Makes decisions primarily based on configuration (desired state) rather than the current state\n- **Output**: An execution graph ready for the plan walk\n\n#### ApplyGraphBuilder\n- **Purpose**: Constructs the execution graph for applying a previously-generated plan\n- **Input**: Configuration, change set (diff), current state, plan variables\n- **Key Difference**: Operates from the plan diff rather than configuration, ensuring only changes explicitly in the plan are applied\n- **Protection Mechanism**: Prevents unintended modifications to resources not in the diff\n\n#### BasicGraphBuilder\n- **Purpose**: Generic graph construction framework\n- **Mechanism**: Executes a series of graph transformations (steps) sequentially\n- **Validation**: Automatically validates the resulting DAG is acyclic and valid\n\n### 1.3 Graph Transformation Pipeline\n\nGraph transformers modify and augment the graph through multiple sequential transformation steps. The pipeline follows a **visitor pattern** where each transformer can add nodes, remove nodes, and create edges.\n\n#### Core Transformation Types:\n\n**Configuration Transformers:**\n- `TransformConfig`: Attaches configuration blocks to resource nodes\n- `TransformProvider`: Adds provider configuration nodes\n- `TransformModule`: Handles module expansion and nesting\n\n**Reference Resolution:**\n- `TransformReference`: Analyzes code expressions to discover dependencies\n  - Identifies references from one resource to another\n  - Analyzes references in provisioner blocks, lifecycle rules, and count/for_each expressions\n  - Creates edges based on discovered dependencies\n\n**State and Schema Attachment:**\n- `TransformAttachState`: Attaches current state snapshots to nodes\n- `TransformAttachSchema`: Binds provider schemas to resource nodes\n- `TransformAttachConfigProvider`: Associates provider configurations with resources\n\n**Dynamic Expansion:**\n- `TransformExpand`: Handles resource instance expansion\n- `TransformResourceCount`: Expands resources with `count` meta-argument\n- `TransformModuleExpansion`: Expands module instances\n\n**Lifecycle Management:**\n- `TransformDestroyEdge`: Adds destruction dependency edges\n- `TransformDestroyPlannedDestroyCBD`: Handles create-before-destroy lifecycle rules\n- `TransformTransitiveReduction`: Optimizes graph by removing redundant transitive edges\n\n**Deferred Actions:**\n- `TransformDeferred`: Marks actions that cannot be evaluated as deferred\n\n**Orphan Handling:**\n- `TransformOrphanResource`: Identifies and marks removed resources for deletion\n- `TransformOrphanOutput`: Handles outputs removed from configuration\n\n### 1.4 Graph Node Types and Interfaces\n\nNodes in the graph represent different infrastructure objects and their operations:\n\n#### Base Node Types:\n- **Resources**: `nodeExpandPlannableResource`, `nodeResourcePlanInstance`, `nodeResourceApplyInstance`\n- **Modules**: `nodeModuleExpand`, represents module instantiation\n- **Providers**: `nodeProviderConfig`, manages provider initialization\n- **Variables**: `nodeRootVariable`, `nodeModuleVariable`, `nodeVariableValidation`\n- **Outputs**: `nodeOutput`, computes and manages outputs\n- **Locals**: `nodeLocal`, evaluates local values\n\n#### Node Interface Contracts:\n\n**GraphNodeReferenceable**\n```go\ntype GraphNodeReferenceable interface {\n    ReferenceableAddrs() []addrs.Referenceable\n}\n```\nIndicates that a node can be referenced by other nodes in expressions.\n\n**GraphNodeReferencer**\n```go\ntype GraphNodeReferencer interface {\n    References() []*addrs.Reference\n}\n```\nProvides the list of resources/variables this node depends on by analyzing its expressions.\n\n**GraphNodeDynamicExpandable**\nIndicates the node expands into multiple sub-nodes (e.g., count expansion):\n- Expansion happens during graph traversal\n- Sub-nodes are created dynamically based on variable values\n- Critical for handling `count` and `for_each` meta-arguments\n\n**GraphNodeExecutable**\n```go\ntype GraphNodeExecutable interface {\n    Execute(EvalContext, walkOperation) tfdiags.Diagnostics\n}\n```\nNodes implementing this interface contain the actual logic executed during graph walks.\n\n### 1.5 Dependency Discovery\n\nDependencies are discovered through multiple mechanisms:\n\n1. **Expression Analysis**: Terraform parses all HCL expressions to identify references\n   - Resource attribute references: `aws_instance.example.id`\n   - Data source references: `data.aws_ami.example.id`\n   - Variable references: `var.instance_type`\n   - Local value references: `local.common_tags`\n\n2. **Explicit Dependencies**: The `depends_on` meta-argument creates explicit dependency edges\n   - Used when implicit dependencies cannot be discovered\n   - Analyzed by `TransformReference` and `graphNodeDependsOn` interface\n\n3. **Provider Dependencies**: Implicit dependencies on provider configurations\n   - Every resource depends on its configured provider node\n   - Ensures providers are initialized before resource operations\n\n4. **Lifecycle Dependencies**: Special edges for create-before-destroy operations\n   - New resource must be created before old is destroyed\n   - Handled by `TransformDestroyEdge` and CBD transformers\n\n## 2. Provider Interface and Lifecycle\n\n### 2.1 Provider Plugin Architecture\n\nTerraform uses a **plugin-based architecture** for providers, communicating via **gRPC** with provider processes:\n\n#### Initialization Flow:\n1. **Plugin Loading**: Providers are loaded from disk or network based on lock file\n2. **Process Launch**: Each unique provider gets its own process\n3. **Handshake**: Terraform and provider negotiate protocol version\n4. **Schema Retrieval**: Terraform fetches resource types and data sources offered\n\n### 2.2 Provider Instance Management\n\n#### contextPlugins Structure:\nMaintains lifecycle of all provider instances across an operation:\n```\ncontextPlugins\n├── factories: map[addrs.Provider]providers.Factory\n├── instances: map[addrs.AbsProviderConfig]providers.Interface\n└── schemas: map[addrs.AbsProviderConfig]providers.ProviderSchema\n```\n\n#### Provider Lifecycle Phases:\n\n**1. Initialization (Plan/Apply Start)**\n- `EvalContext.InitProvider()` creates provider instance\n- Called once per provider configuration\n- Establishes gRPC connection to provider process\n- Panics if provider already initialized (prevents accidental duplicate initialization)\n\n**2. Configuration (Before Operations)**\n- `EvalContext.ConfigureProvider()` passes HCL configuration to provider\n- Configuration merged from:\n  - Explicit `provider` blocks\n  - User input via `provider_input()` function\n  - Environment variables\n- Returns diagnostics if configuration invalid\n\n**3. Operation**\n- Provider instance available via `EvalContext.Provider(addr)`\n- Returns `providers.Interface` implementing operations:\n  - `GetSchema()`: Retrieve resource/data source schemas\n  - `ValidateResourceConfig()`: Validate resource configuration\n  - `PlanResourceChange()`: Determine what will change\n  - `ApplyResourceChange()`: Execute the change\n  - `ReadDataSource()`: Fetch data source values\n  - Several other operations for refresh, import, etc.\n\n**4. Cleanup**\n- `EvalContext.CloseProvider()` closes provider connection\n- Called when provider no longer needed\n- Releases gRPC connection and cleans up resources\n\n### 2.3 Resource Operation Delegation\n\nResource operations are delegated through a standard flow:\n\n#### Plan Time (Provider Methods):\n1. **ReadResource**: Fetch current remote state for comparison\n2. **PlanResourceChange**: Provider determines what action is needed\n   - Inputs: prior state, proposed new config, config marks\n   - Outputs: planned new state, action (create/update/delete/no-op)\n   - Provider implements custom logic to detect drift\n\n#### Apply Time (Provider Methods):\n1. **ApplyResourceChange**: Execute the planned action\n   - Inputs: prior state, planned state\n   - Outputs: final state after operation\n   - Provider handles all cloud API interactions\n2. **ReadResource**: Verify final state was achieved\n\n### 2.4 Provider Configuration Resolution\n\nProvider configuration involves multiple layers of merging:\n\n**Configuration Sources (in precedence order):**\n1. **Explicit `provider` blocks**: Hardcoded in configuration\n2. **User input**: From `provider_input()` interactive prompts\n3. **Environment variables**: Provider-specific env var support\n4. **Defaults**: Provider applies default values\n\n**Multi-alias Providers:**\n- Single provider type can be configured multiple times with different aliases\n- Each configuration creates separate provider instance\n- Resources select provider via `provider = \"type.alias\"` argument\n\n### 2.5 Provider Schema Management\n\nProvider schemas are cached to avoid repeated round-trips:\n\n- **PreloadedProviderSchemas**: Optional optimization in `ContextOpts`\n  - Caller can pre-load schemas already fetched elsewhere\n  - Avoids redundant provider initialization\n  - Must be accurate or runtime behavior becomes erratic\n\n- **Schema Caching**: Schemas cached per provider configuration address\n  - Retrieved via `EvalContext.ProviderSchema(addr)`\n  - Used for configuration validation and type checking\n  - Critical for error messages and expression evaluation\n\n## 3. State Management\n\n### 3.1 State Structure\n\nTerraform maintains a hierarchical state representation:\n\n```\nState\n├── Version: int (schema version)\n├── Serial: int (monotonically incrementing change counter)\n├── Lineage: string (tracks state file identity across snapshots)\n├── Modules: map[addrs.ModuleInstance]Module\n│   └── Module (per module instance)\n│       ├── Outputs: map[string]OutputValue\n│       ├── Resources: map[string]Resource\n│       │   └── Resource\n│       │       └── Instances: map[addrs.InstanceKey]InstanceObject\n│       │           └── InstanceObject (per resource instance)\n│       │               ├── Status: (Ready, Tainted, Deposed)\n│       │               ├── AttrsJSON: []byte (actual resource attributes)\n│       │               ├── Private: []byte (provider-specific metadata)\n│       │               └── Deposed: []DeposedInstanceObject (for CBD)\n│       └── (nested modules)\n└── CheckResults: map[addrs.AbsResourceInstance]CheckState\n```\n\n**Key Concepts:**\n- **Status**: Indicates health of instance (Ready, Tainted, Deposed)\n- **AttrsJSON**: Resource attributes stored as JSON for schema-agnostic access\n- **Private**: Provider-specific data opaque to Terraform core\n- **Deposed**: Previous version kept during create-before-destroy operations\n- **CheckResults**: Results from resource checks (assertions)\n\n### 3.2 State Snapshots and Concurrency\n\nState access during graph walks is carefully managed:\n\n**Snapshot Mechanism:**\n- State is snapshotted at the beginning of walks\n- Nodes read from the snapshot, not live state\n- Prevents inconsistencies from concurrent updates\n- Final state computed at walk end and persisted\n\n**Synchronization:**\n- `states.SyncState` wrapper adds locking\n- Prevents concurrent modification of in-memory state\n- Serializes writes while allowing multiple readers\n\n**Concurrency Model:**\n- Multiple graph nodes execute in parallel\n- Each node reads snapshot, computes changes, queues writes\n- After all nodes complete, writes applied atomically\n- Results in consistent state even with parallel execution\n\n### 3.3 State Changes During Plan and Apply\n\n#### Plan Phase:\n1. **Refresh**: Fetch current remote values (if not skipped)\n   - Provider.ReadResource() called for each managed resource\n   - Current remote state becomes prior state for comparison\n2. **Plan**: Compare prior state with configuration\n   - Provider.PlanResourceChange() determines action needed\n   - Actions: Create, Read, Update, Delete, No-op, Replace\n3. **Plan File**: Actions serialized to plan file for apply phase\n   - Includes configuration, prior state, proposed changes\n   - Enables apply phase to reproduce plan consistently\n\n#### Apply Phase:\n1. **Validation**: Verify plan still applies to current state\n   - Checks for external changes since plan creation\n   - Detects conflicts that would invalidate the plan\n2. **Application**: Execute planned changes\n   - Provider.ApplyResourceChange() performs operations\n   - State updated with new values from provider\n3. **Persistence**: Final state written to state file\n   - Serial incremented\n   - Lineage preserved for tracking\n   - Stored in backend (local, remote, etc.)\n\n### 3.4 State Backends and Remote Storage\n\nState backends provide persistence mechanisms:\n\n**Backend Responsibilities:**\n- **State Lock**: Prevent concurrent modifications\n  - `StateLocker` interface provides distributed locks\n  - Critical for teams sharing infrastructure\n  - Ensures serial execution of operations\n- **Remote Persistence**: Store state durably\n  - S3, Terraform Cloud, Azure Storage, etc.\n  - Backup and version control built-in\n- **State Versioning**: Track state history\n  - Backend may keep historical versions\n  - Enables recovery from accidental changes\n\n**State Manager (`statemgr.State`):**\n- Wraps backend storage\n- Implements atomic read/write semantics\n- Handles lock acquisition before writes\n\n## 4. Execution Flow and Hook System\n\n### 4.1 Overall Execution Pipeline\n\n#### Command Invocation → Plan/Apply Cycle:\n\n```\nUser Command (terraform plan/apply)\n    ↓\nLoad Configuration\n    ↓\nLoad Prior State\n    ↓\nCreate Context\n    ├─ Initialize Plugins\n    ├─ Configure Providers\n    └─ Set up Evaluation Context\n    ↓\nBuild Graph (Plan-specific or Apply-specific)\n    ├─ Apply Transformations\n    ├─ Discover Dependencies\n    ├─ Expand Resources\n    └─ Validate DAG\n    ↓\nWalk Graph (Concurrent Execution)\n    ├─ Topological Ordering\n    ├─ Node Evaluation\n    ├─ Provider Operations\n    └─ State Updates\n    ↓\nReturn Results\n    ├─ Plan: Serialized change set\n    └─ Apply: Updated state\n```\n\n### 4.2 Graph Walking and Execution\n\n#### Walker Pattern\n\nThe **GraphWalker** interface orchestrates execution:\n\n```go\ntype GraphWalker interface {\n    EvalContext() EvalContext\n    enterScope(evalContextScope) EvalContext\n    exitScope(evalContextScope)\n    Execute(EvalContext, GraphNodeExecutable) tfdiags.Diagnostics\n}\n```\n\n**Responsibilities:**\n- Provides evaluation context to nodes\n- Manages scope entry/exit for module traversal\n- Executes individual nodes via `Execute()`\n- Aggregates diagnostics\n\n#### ContextGraphWalker Implementation:\n\nThe main implementation coordinating actual work:\n\n**EvalContext Provisioning:**\n- Creates evaluation context per node\n- Context provides access to:\n  - Current module path\n  - State, configuration, variables\n  - Provider instances\n  - Hook callbacks\n  - Expression evaluation\n\n**Node Execution:**\n1. Determines if node is executable (`GraphNodeExecutable`)\n2. Calls `Execute()` method with eval context\n3. Collects and aggregates diagnostics\n4. Propagates panics with context\n\n**Parallelism Control:**\n- `parallelSem`: Semaphore limiting concurrent executions\n- Default parallelism: 10 (configurable)\n- Limits CPU pressure and provider rate throttling\n- Each node acquisition blocks until semaphore permits\n\n#### DAG Walk Implementation\n\nThe `dag.Walk()` function implements parallel graph traversal:\n\n**Algorithm:**\n1. **Topological Sort**: Vertices with no dependencies ready first\n2. **Async Dispatch**: Ready vertices sent to worker goroutines\n3. **Dependency Tracking**: Maintains in-flight work count\n4. **Completion Notifications**: Workers signal completion\n5. **Deadlock Detection**: Identifies cycles and halts\n6. **Aggregation**: Collects all diagnostics\n\n**Key Properties:**\n- Fully asynchronous, maximizes parallelism\n- Respects dependency edges\n- Stops on first error (with graceful shutdown of in-flight nodes)\n- Provides panic safety through recovery handlers\n\n### 4.3 Hook System and Extension Points\n\nHooks provide extension points for observing and controlling execution:\n\n#### Hook Interface\n\n```go\ntype Hook interface {\n    // Plan/Apply lifecycle\n    PreApply(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, action, priorState, plannedNewState) (HookAction, error)\n    PostApply(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, newState, err error) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // Plan comparison (diff)\n    PreDiff(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, priorState, proposedNewState) (HookAction, error)\n    PostDiff(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, action, priorState, plannedNewState) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // Refresh operations\n    PreRefresh(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, priorState) (HookAction, error)\n    PostRefresh(id HookResourceIdentity, dk DeposedKey, priorState, newState) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // Provisioning\n    PreProvisionInstance(id HookResourceIdentity, state) (HookAction, error)\n    PostProvisionInstance(id HookResourceIdentity, state) (HookAction, error)\n    PreProvisionInstanceStep(id HookResourceIdentity, typeName) (HookAction, error)\n    PostProvisionInstanceStep(id HookResourceIdentity, typeName, err) (HookAction, error)\n    ProvisionOutput(id HookResourceIdentity, typeName, line string)\n\n    // Import operations\n    PreImportState(id HookResourceIdentity, importID) (HookAction, error)\n    PostImportState(id HookResourceIdentity, imported []ImportedResource) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // Action invocations (Terraform 1.10+)\n    StartAction(id HookActionIdentity) (HookAction, error)\n    ProgressAction(id HookActionIdentity, progress string) (HookAction, error)\n    CompleteAction(id HookActionIdentity, err error) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // State updates\n    PostStateUpdate(new *states.State) (HookAction, error)\n\n    // Shutdown signal\n    Stopping()\n}\n```\n\n#### Hook Action Control\n\n```go\ntype HookAction byte\n\nconst (\n    HookActionContinue HookAction = iota  // Continue normal execution\n    HookActionHalt                         // Halt immediately, cancel operation\n)\n```\n\nHooks can:\n- **Observe**: Log operations, collect metrics\n- **Control**: Return errors to halt execution\n- **Modify**: Update context during execution (in some hooks)\n- **Respond**: React to state updates\n\n#### Hook Registration and Invocation\n\n1. **Registration**: Hooks passed in `ContextOpts` during Context creation\n2. **Storage**: Stored in Context along with stop hook\n3. **Invocation**: Called via `EvalContext.Hook()` during node execution\n4. **Ordering**: All hooks called for each event (in registration order)\n5. **Aggregation**: First error halts execution, other hooks not called\n\n#### Internal Hooks\n\n**stopHook**: Built-in hook detecting graceful shutdown signals\n- Monitors SIGINT, SIGTERM signals\n- Sets stop context causing nodes to abort\n\n#### Use Cases\n\n- **UI Callbacks**: Update progress bars and status displays\n- **Logging**: Detailed operation logging\n- **Metrics**: Collect performance data\n- **Policy**: Enforce constraints (e.g., prevent certain resources)\n- **Testing**: Mock provider responses\n\n### 4.4 Plan-Specific Execution\n\n**PlanGraphBuilder** creates graph specific to planning:\n\n1. **Refresh**: Fetch current state (unless skipped)\n2. **Config Comparison**: Compare current with desired\n3. **Plan Generation**: Provider determines actions\n4. **Deferral Support**: Mark unplannable resources as deferred\n\n**Node Types Used:**\n- `nodeExpandPlannableResource`: Expansion for resources\n- `nodeResourcePlanInstance`: Planning for each instance\n- `nodeProviderConfig`: Provider initialization\n- `nodeRootVariable`: Variable evaluation\n- `nodeOutput`: Output value computation\n\n**Outputs:**\n- `plans.Plan` containing:\n  - Computed changes per resource\n  - Prior state snapshot\n  - Configuration snapshot\n  - Root variable values\n  - Timestamp for audit trail\n\n### 4.5 Apply-Specific Execution\n\n**ApplyGraphBuilder** creates graph specific to applying:\n\n1. **Validation**: Verify plan is still applicable\n2. **Provisioning**: Run provisioners after resources created\n3. **Application**: Execute provider operations\n4. **State Persistence**: Write updated state\n\n**Node Types Used:**\n- `nodeExpandApplyableResource`: Resource expansion\n- `nodeResourceApplyInstance`: Applying each instance\n- `nodeProviderConfig`: Provider initialization\n- `nodeProvisioner`: Provisioning execution\n\n**Key Differences from Plan:**\n- Operates on plan diff, not full configuration\n- Provider apply operations modify infrastructure\n- State written durably after completion\n- Provisions executed post-creation (if configured)\n\n### 4.6 Dynamic Expansion (count/for_each)\n\nResource instances are expanded dynamically during graph traversal:\n\n**Expansion Nodes:**\n- `nodeExpandPlannableResource` (plan phase)\n- `nodeExpandApplyableResource` (apply phase)\n\n**Mechanism:**\n1. **Parent Node**: Represents resource as a whole\n2. **Expansion Decision**: Evaluates `count` or `for_each` expression\n3. **Sub-node Creation**: Generates instance-specific nodes\n4. **Dynamic Dependency**: Instance sub-nodes added to graph dynamically\n5. **Parallel Execution**: Instances can execute in parallel\n\n**Dependency Propagation:**\n- Dependencies on unexpanded resource apply to all instances\n- Dependencies on specific instances specified via splat expressions\n- Inter-instance dependencies via implicit references\n\n### 4.7 Error Handling and Propagation\n\n**Diagnostic Aggregation:**\n- Each node execution returns `tfdiags.Diagnostics`\n- Collected in graph walk, returned to caller\n- Includes errors, warnings, and informational messages\n\n**Error Severity Levels:**\n- **Error**: Fatal, prevents apply execution\n- **Warning**: Non-fatal, operation continues\n- **Info**: Informational, for user awareness\n\n**Panic Safety:**\n- Panic in node execution caught and logged\n- Graph walk continues with other nodes\n- Panic converted to diagnostic error\n- Prevents entire process from crashing\n\n**Graceful Shutdown:**\n- On error, graph walk signals remaining nodes\n- Nodes check stop context, abort cleanly\n- In-flight operations completed or cancelled\n- Resources in partial states logged for recovery\n\n## 5. Component Integration\n\n### 5.1 Context as Central Coordinator\n\nThe `Context` struct serves as the central coordinator:\n\n```go\ntype Context struct {\n    meta *ContextMeta              // Working directory metadata\n    plugins *contextPlugins        // Provider/provisioner factories\n    hooks []Hook                   // Extension points\n    parallelSem Semaphore         // Concurrency control\n    runContext context.Context     // Cancellation support\n    // ...\n}\n```\n\n**Responsibilities:**\n- Owns plugins and hooks\n- Controls parallelism\n- Manages operation lifecycle\n- Coordinates graph building and walking\n\n### 5.2 EvalContext as State and Service Provider\n\nThe `EvalContext` provides all services nodes need:\n\n- **State Access**: Current and proposed state values\n- **Configuration**: Module configurations and variable values\n- **Provider Access**: Initialize, configure, and use providers\n- **Hook Dispatch**: Call registered hooks\n- **Expression Evaluation**: Evaluate HCL expressions\n- **Module Path**: Current position in module hierarchy\n\nImplemented by `ContextGraphWalker` during execution.\n\n### 5.3 Graph as Execution Plan\n\nThe `Graph` struct represents the execution plan:\n\n- **Vertices**: Individual infrastructure objects and operations\n- **Edges**: Dependency relationships\n- **Validation**: Ensures DAG structure (no cycles)\n- **Walking**: Traversal with node execution\n\n### 5.4 Plan as Bridge Between Phases\n\nThe `plans.Plan` struct bridges planning and application:\n\n- **Captures**: What will change and why\n- **Immutable**: Prevents accidental change between phases\n- **Serializable**: Can be reviewed, stored, transmitted\n- **Reproducible**: Apply reproduces plan consistently\n\n## 6. Advanced Topics\n\n### 6.1 Targeted Operations\n\n**Mechanism:**\n- User specifies resources to target via `-target` flag\n- `Targets` list passed to graph builders\n- `TransformTargets` removes untargeted resources from graph\n- Warnings issued that plan is incomplete\n\n**Use Cases:**\n- Rescue partial failures\n- Manage dependencies manually\n- Operations on single resources\n- **Warning**: Can cause inconsistencies if dependencies crossed\n\n### 6.2 Create-Before-Destroy (CBD)\n\n**Lifecycle Rule** for zero-downtime updates:\n\n1. **Detection**: Resource has `lifecycle { create_before_destroy = true }`\n2. **Graph Modification**: `TransformDestroyEdge` reorders dependencies\n3. **Execution**:\n   - New resource created first\n   - Old resource destroyed after new is ready\n   - Prevents service interruption\n\n### 6.3 Deferred Actions\n\n**Purpose**: Handle situations where operations cannot be planned yet\n\n**Triggers:**\n- Unknown values in `count` or `for_each` expressions\n- Provider-issued deferral notices\n- Data source dependencies on managed resources being created\n\n**Handling:**\n- Marked as deferred in plan\n- Not executed during this apply\n- Requires subsequent plan/apply to complete\n- Stored in state for next run\n\n### 6.4 Ephemeral Resources\n\n**Purpose**: Temporary resources that don't persist in state\n\n**Characteristics:**\n- Data source-like but for specific use cases\n- Values only exist during operation\n- Not stored in state\n- Recreated each plan/apply\n\n**Lifecycle:**\n- Opened at start of operation\n- Used by dependent resources\n- Closed at operation completion\n\n### 6.5 Resource Importing\n\n**Purpose**: Bring existing infrastructure under Terraform management\n\n**Flow:**\n- User specifies resource address and import ID\n- `nodeResourceImport` fetches remote state\n- Provider's `ReadResource` populates state with remote values\n- Configuration must match or manual editing required\n- State written for use in subsequent plans\n\n## Conclusion\n\nTerraform's plan/apply pipeline is a sophisticated system balancing:\n\n- **Correctness**: DAGs ensure proper ordering\n- **Performance**: Parallel execution maximizes throughput\n- **Reliability**: State snapshots and atomic updates ensure consistency\n- **Extensibility**: Hooks and interfaces enable customization\n- **Safety**: Immutable plans enable review before execution\n\nUnderstanding these architectural components is essential for:\n- Extending Terraform with custom providers\n- Building tools that invoke Terraform programmatically\n- Debugging complex infrastructure changes\n- Contributing to Terraform core development\n- Optimizing Terraform for specific use cases\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

This document provides a detailed technical explanation of how these components interact to execute infrastructure changes.\n     6→\n     7→## 1. Graph Builder System\n     8→\n     9→### 1.1 Graph Architecture\n    10→\n    11→The Terraform execution model is built around a **Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG)** that represents resources, data sources, modules, and their dependencies. This document provides a detailed technical explanation of how these components interact to execute infrastructure changes.\n\n## 1. Graph Builder System\n\n### 1.1 Graph Architecture\n\nThe Terraform execution model is built around a **Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG)** that represents resources, data sources, modules, and their dependencies. This graph is the foundation of Terraform's execution model and enables:\n\n- **Concurrent execution** of independent nodes\n- **Dependency ordering** to ensure correct sequencing\n- **Partial execution** through targeted operations\n- **Resource expansion** for count/for_each resources\n\n### 1.2 Graph Builders\n\nTerraform provides specialized graph builders for different operation types:\n\n#### PlanGraphBuilder\n- **Purpose**: Constructs the execution graph for planning operations\n- **Input**: Current configuration, prior state, root module variables\n- **Key Fields**:\n  - `Config`: The desired infrastructure configuration\n  - `State`: Current infrastructure state\n  - `RootVariableValues`: User-provided variable inputs\n  - `Targets`: Optional resource filtering for targeted operations\n  - `ForceReplace`: Resource instances that must be replaced\n  - `ExternalProviderConfigs`: Pre-initialized provider instances\n\n- **Operation**: Makes decisions primarily based on configuration (desired state) rather than the current state\n- **Output**: An execution graph ready for the plan walk\n\n#### ApplyGraphBuilder\n- **Purpose**: Constructs the execution graph for applying a previously-generated plan\n- **Input**: Configuration, change set (diff), current state, plan variables\n- **Key Difference**: Operates from the plan diff rather than configuration, ensuring only changes explicitly in the plan are applied\n- **Protection Mechanism**: Prevents unintended modifications to resources not in the diff\n\n#### BasicGraphBuilder\n- **Purpose**: Generic graph construction framework\n- **Mechanism**: Executes a series of graph transformations (steps) sequentially\n- **Validation**: Automatically validates the resulting DAG is acyclic and valid\n\n### 1.3 Graph Transformation Pipeline\n\nGraph transformers modify and augment the graph through multiple sequential transformation steps. The pipeline follows a **visitor pattern** where each transformer can add nodes, remove nodes, and create edges.\n\n#### Core Transformation Types:\n\n**Configuration Transformers:**\n- `TransformConfig`: Attaches configuration blocks to resource nodes\n- `TransformProvider`: Adds provider configuration nodes\n- `TransformModule`: Handles module expansion and nesting\n\n**Reference Resolution:**\n- `TransformReference`: Analyzes code expressions to discover dependencies\n  - Identifies references from one resource to another\n  - Analyzes references in provisioner blocks, lifecycle rules, and count/for_each expressions\n  - Creates edges based on discovered dependencies\n\n**State and Schema Attachment:**\n- `TransformAttachState`: Attaches current state snapshots to nodes\n- `TransformAttachSchema`: Binds provider schemas to resource nodes\n- `TransformAttachConfigProvider`: Associates provider configurations with resources\n\n**Dynamic Expansion:**\n- `TransformExpand`: Handles resource instance expansion\n- `TransformResourceCount`: Expands resources with `count` meta-argument\n- `TransformModuleExpansion`: Expands module instances\n\n**Lifecycle Management:**\n- `TransformDestroyEdge`: Adds destruction dependency edges\n- `TransformDestroyPlannedDestroyCBD`: Handles create-before-destroy lifecycle rules\n- `TransformTransitiveReduction`: Optimizes graph by removing redundant transitive edges\n\n**Deferred Actions:**\n- `TransformDeferred`: Marks actions that cannot be evaluated as deferred\n\n**Orphan Handling:**\n- `TransformOrphanResource`: Identifies and marks removed resources for deletion\n- `TransformOrphanOutput`: Handles outputs removed from configuration\n\n### 1.4 Graph Node Types and Interfaces\n\nNodes in the graph represent different infrastructure objects and their operations:\n\n#### Base Node Types:\n- **Resources**: `nodeExpandPlannableResource`, `nodeResourcePlanInstance`, `nodeResourceApplyInstance`\n- **Modules**: `nodeModuleExpand`, represents module instantiation\n- **Providers**: `nodeProviderConfig`, manages provider initialization\n- **Variables**: `nodeRootVariable`, `nodeModuleVariable`, `nodeVariableValidation`\n- **Outputs**: `nodeOutput`, computes and manages outputs\n- **Locals**: `nodeLocal`, evaluates local values\n\n#### Node Interface Contracts:\n\n**GraphNodeReferenceable**","numLines":100,"startLine":1,"totalLines":762}}}
